# Tools

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

} // namespace functions<|end|><|start|>user<|message|>Instructions: Answer the following question about drug properties.

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). type get_scaffold = (_: {

   - **H‑bond acceptors (HBA)**: 3 (3 carbonyl oxygen atoms; the imide nitrogen is not counted as an H‑bond donor).  
   - **LogP**: ≈ 1.5‑2.0 (estimated from the phenyl ring plus two lactam carbonyls).  
   - **Topological polar surface area (tPSA)**: ≈ 40‑50 Å² (based on three acceptor oxygens).  
   - **Rotatable bonds**: 0–1 (rigid bicyclic scaffold).  

2. **Rule‑based assessment**  
   - **Lipinski**: MW < 500 Da, HBA ≤ 5, HBD = 0, logP < 5 – all satisfied.  
   - **BBB‐permeation**: Typical thresholds are:  
     - logP < 5, tPSA < 90 Å², HBD ≤ 2, MW < 500 Da.  
     - This compound comfortably fits within all these limits, especially having no H‑bond donors and a relatively low tPSA.

3. **Comparative examples**  
   - **Phthalimide** (similar imide structure) has tPSA ≈ 43 Å² and readily crosses the BBB.  
   - **Carbamazepine** (also contains an imide motif) has logP ≈ 2.5 and is a known CNS drug.  
   - The given molecule is even smaller and lacks additional polar groups, suggesting better permeability.

4. **Conclusion**  
   The physicochemical profile indicates that the molecule should be able to passively diffuse across the BBB.

**Answer:** **(B)**  (crosses the BBB)<|return|>

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

# Tools

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

} // namespace functions<|end|><|start|>user<|message|>Instructions: Answer the following question about drug properties.

# Tools

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

} // namespace functions<|end|><|start|>user<|message|>Instructions: Answer the following question about drug properties.

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {

task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. type get_scaffold = (_: {

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

}) => any;

# Tools

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).

}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string

type find_similar_molecules = (_: {

k?: number
}) => any;

type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. type get_electronic_properties = (_: {

type get_scaffold = (_: {

## functions

namespace functions {

// Get a comprehensive molecular profile including identity (MW), drug-likeness (QED, Lipinski violations), key physicochemical properties (logP, TPSA, HBD, HBA, rotatable bonds, Fsp3, molar refractivity), charge information, complexity metrics (Bertz CT, stereocenters), topology indices (Hall-Kier, Kappa, Balaban J, IPC), and pharmacophore feature counts (hydrophobic, aromatic, ionizable, Zn-binder).
type get_molecule_profile = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze functional groups in a molecule using AccFG. Returns named functional groups with fragment SMILES, attachment points, and atom IDs for structural reasoning. Use this to understand what chemical motifs are present.
type analyze_functional_groups = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Analyze ring topology and aromaticity. Includes fused ring system analysis, PAH detection (≥3 fused aromatic rings), macrocycle detection, ring type breakdown (aromatic/aliphatic/saturated/heterocyclic), and aromaticity metrics.
type analyze_ring_systems = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Assess ADME properties: pKa prediction (acidic/basic sites), ionization state classification at target pH, logD estimation, and aqueous solubility. These are causally linked: pKa → ionization → logD → permeability.
type assess_adme_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string,
// Target pH for ionization/logD estimation (default: 7.4).
ph?: number
}) => any;

// Compute 3D conformational properties from a multi-conformer ensemble. Returns Boltzmann-weighted exposed polar surface area (ePSA), polar fraction, and 3D shape descriptors (PMI ratios for rod/disc/sphere classification, radius of gyration, asphericity, eccentricity, spherocity). Computationally expensive (~seconds). Use only when 3D surface/shape information is specifically needed beyond 2D TPSA.
type get_3d_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Screen a molecule for toxicophores — structural motifs associated with specific toxicity mechanisms. Groups alerts into categories like 'Michael acceptor' (protein-reactive electrophile), 'alkylating agent' (DNA-reactive), 'PAH' (CYP-activated), etc. Each category includes a mechanistic explanation. Also screens against ToxAlerts endpoint-specific patterns covering skin sensitization, mutagenicity, carcinogenicity, reactive metabolite formation, electrophilic reactivity, chelation, and promiscuity. Returns pharmacophore feature counts (donor/acceptor/aromatic/hydrophobe). Use this to identify structural liabilities and understand their toxicological mechanisms.
type screen_toxicophores = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Find the most similar molecules from the training set using task-aware embeddings. Returns K nearest neighbors with their labels and functional groups, plus the closest molecule with the opposite label for contrastive SAR reasoning. Includes KNN accuracy metrics (global and local neighborhood). Use this for structure-activity analysis.
type find_similar_molecules = (_: {
// SMILES string of the query molecule.
smiles: string,
// Task name for task-aware retrieval (e.g., 'AMES', 'DILI').
task: string,
// Number of nearest neighbors to retrieve (default: 5).
k?: number
}) => any;

// Remove salts and counterions from a SMILES string, keeping only the largest organic fragment. Use as a preprocessing step before other analyses when the input SMILES contains disconnected components separated by '.' that include inorganic ions (e.g., [Na+], Cl-, etc.).
type remove_salts = (_: {
// SMILES string (may contain salts).
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Evaluate a simple arithmetic expression. Supports basic math operations (+, -, *, /) and functions: exp, log, sqrt, sin, cos, round.
type evaluate_arithmetic = (_: {
// The arithmetic expression to evaluate.
expression: string
}) => any;

// Compute electronic properties: HOMO/LUMO energies and gap, dipole moment, electrophilicity index. Uses GFN2-xTB semi-empirical quantum chemistry if available, otherwise falls back to RDKit Gasteiger charges. HOMO-LUMO gap indicates reactivity (small gap → more reactive → potential mutagenicity). Dipole moment correlates with solubility and membrane permeability.
type get_electronic_properties = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Predict CYP450 metabolism: returns top 3 ranked metabolites with SMILES, reaction types, and priority scores for Phase 1 (oxidation, reduction, hydrolysis) and Phase 2 (glucuronidation, sulfation, GSH conjugation). Uses GLORYx (FAME3 SoM + reaction rules) when cached, otherwise SyGMa rule-based metabolite prediction as fallback. 
type predict_metabolism_sites = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;

// Extract the Murcko scaffold (core ring+linker skeleton with side chains stripped) and generic framework (pure topological skeleton). Shows what fraction of the molecule is scaffold vs. side chains. Use this to identify the core structural class of a molecule and compare scaffolds across molecules.
type get_scaffold = (_: {
// SMILES string of the molecule.
smiles: string
}) => any;
